LLaDA-8B-Instruct · 300k continuation checkpoint · 100 existing cases

New masking checkpoint
100 TSP20 cases · 256 calls

IMPORTANT LIMITATION · BOTH TRAINED MASKING HEADS

TSP-only head and decoder: cannot answer general questions

This applies to the head trained in the 500k experiment and its additional 300k continuation. Both require city coordinates and route positions. The tested decoder supplies a fixed tour format and restricts predictions to city IDs. These heads do not provide general-purpose token remasking.

Underlying LLaDA + LoRA: the same weights can be run with ordinary text decoding, with the TSP head bypassed. That is a different generation procedure. These experiments did not evaluate general-question quality through ordinary decoding.

The reported TSP scores include this specialized decoder; they are not evidence of general-purpose chat with the head active.

Generate, mask, reconstruct, and keep the latest result. This test allows worse and invalid routes to become the next input, for up to 256 total model calls.

What ran

  1. The trained model generated the initial route in 16 diffusion passes.
  2. One backbone/head pass scored the 54 trained contiguous masks of 5–8 cities, using gain for valid routes and feasibility for invalid routes.
  3. Both digit tokens of each selected city were replaced with the actual mask token.
  4. The model reconstructed the selected fields in up to 16 useful passes, normally 5–8; the final block can be shorter when the call budget runs out.
  5. Every completion became the next input. The loop continued until fewer than two calls remained.

The 256-call cap includes selection and reconstruction. The stop/KEEP threshold was disabled. City 00 and the route format remained fixed; uniqueness was not enforced. Validity remains an existing head feature, but objective values and reference optima never control the trajectory.

This is a fixed-budget stress test of the trained selector and reconstruction model. The head can repeatedly choose the same mask on an unchanged route. It can also disturb an optimal route because this test forces continued masking.

3,088 remasking cycles; 42,258 digit-token mask operations. 2,967 selections repeated the preceding action on the same input, across 100 cases.

79 cases never changed their route. The mean number of distinct complete routes visited was 1.29.

At each cutoff, use the latest fully reconstructed tour at or before that call count. These are snapshots of one run, not extra evaluations.

Mean model calls: 255.98; range 255–256. Mean inference time: 28.88s per case. Four independent L40S replicas processed 25 cases each. Actual allocation: 12.57 minutes and 0.838 GPU-hours.

The remaining budget shortened the final reconstruction block in 63 cases. Those outputs are kept in the final score. Their final-block changes: {'equal length': 55, 'shorter': 4, 'became invalid': 2, 'repaired': 1, 'longer': 1}.

How to interpret the comparison

The initial routes match the previous unfiltered 256-call test in 46/100 cases. The previous run returned 95 valid and 46 optimal tours with mean valid gap 0.699%. Both runs use the same 100 geometries, seeds and unconditional state updates. This run changes the checkpoint and restricts masks to 5–8 cities, so it does not isolate the effect of weights alone. Final-route changes versus the previous checkpoint: {'equal length': 48, 'shorter': 19, 'longer': 26, 'became invalid': 2, 'repaired': 5}.

On the 93 cases with a valid final route under both checkpoints, mean gap was 0.692% previously and 1.754% now. This paired subset avoids changing the validity denominator.

For the new model itself, the 92 cases valid both before and after remasking had mean gap 1.758% before and 1.332% after.

This remains the same historical 100-case benchmark. It measures this decoding procedure on these cases and does not establish performance on a new test distribution.
